# Patch log

Every local modification to a file inherited from upstream OpenCut Classic
(`cf5e79e919144200294fb9fed22a222592a0aeea`) is recorded here. The pinned upstream commit must stay
identifiable as the baseline, so a reader can reconstruct exactly what this fork changed and why.

> **This table must be appended to by every task that modifies an upstream file — as the change is
> made, not retroactively.** A modified upstream file with no row here is a defect. New files added
> by this fork are not patches and are not listed; see `UPSTREAM.md` for the added-file inventory.

Verify the set of modified files against the pin with:

```
git diff --name-only cf5e79e919144200294fb9fed22a222592a0aeea -- apps rust script
```

Columns:

- **id** — stable patch identifier (`P-nnn`), referenced from code comments where useful.
- **upstream path** — repo-relative path of the inherited file.
- **change** — what was actually altered.
- **rationale** — why, including the Slice spec clause that forces it. A patch with no forcing
  clause is scope creep.
- **verification** — how the patch was confirmed not to change behaviour (or what behaviour change
  was accepted).

| P-001 | `apps/web/next.config.ts`                                                                | Added `typescript: { ignoreBuildErrors: true }`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | The pinned baseline does not type-check, so `next build` fails at the type gate and no production Next build exists. §3.3 requires a production `next build` of `apps/web` as the **parity reference** the Vite output is compared against, and §3.2 keeps `apps/web` buildable as the live regression check. Skipping the type gate restores that reference without editing any editor source — the alternative (fixing the ~6 upstream type defects) would silently repair the baseline this Slice exists to compare against. See `UPSTREAM.md` § "Known upstream defects" for the full error list.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | `next build` compiles successfully both before and after (the failure was in the post-compile type pass only), so emitted output is unaffected. | P-002 | `.gitignore`                                                                             | Added `.rasen-store/`, `rust/wasm/pkg/`, `apps/vite-example/dist/` and the three parity-run output directories `apps/vite-example/tests/{parity-artifacts,.pw-output,.playwright}/`. **Amended (ruling R10):** the `.rasen-store/` comment previously claimed "`rasen/` itself stays tracked on this branch", which was false — this worktree has no `rasen/` directory. The comment now states what is true: the planning artifacts live in the main checkout as untracked local state, and this branch carries code plus the provenance documents. **Amended again (review MAJ-2):** the parity rule covered `parity-artifacts/` and `.pw-output/` but not `.playwright/`, an earlier output location for the same run, leaving 14 files there committable. **Amended a third time (this change, alongside P-016..P-020):** added `apps/vite-example/tests/probe-artifacts/` and `apps/vite-example/tests/.pw-output-probe/`, the output locations of the new migration and stickers probes driven by `playwright.probe.config.ts`. | `.rasen-store/` is a machine-local store pointer (design D13, as corrected by R10); `rust/wasm/pkg/` is `wasm-pack` build output written by the §3.1 rebuild-correspondence check; `apps/vite-example/dist/` is the Vite production build; the three `tests/` directories are parity-run output, regenerated by `bun run test:parity`. None belongs in version control — the committed outcome of a parity run is `PARITY.md`. Upstream ignores `.next/` in `apps/web/.gitignore` but had no rule covering a second app's build output, because no second app existed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Repository hygiene, and one correctness consequence. Without the `dist/` rule `git status` listed 303 untracked build artifacts (~34 MB, including the 21.6 MB ONNX wasm) as committable. The missing `.playwright/` rule was worse than hygiene: `tests/.playwright/next/snapshot-next.json` was a **superseded** Next snapshot predating the media sort-by-name normalization in `snapshot.ts`, named identically to the real one and one directory away from it, and a valid input to the documented diff command — run against it the differ reports 39 differences / 27 semantic (exit 1), the opposite verdict to the committed `PARITY.md`. The directory has been deleted as well as ignored, and the differ's own usage docstring, which still cited the stale `.playwright/` path, now cites `parity-artifacts/`. Verified after the fix: `git check-ignore -v` matches all three rules, and no path matching `parity-artifacts                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | .playwright | .pw-output | snapshot- | ledger- | results-`appears in`git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard`. `main`is untouched. **Third amendment verified the same way:** before the rule,`git ls-files --others --exclude-standard` listed 18 committable probe artefacts, one of which (`probe-<host>.json`) carries a verbatim copy of a persisted project record — the same leak shape the `.playwright/`rule exists to prevent. After it, that command lists only the four new probe *source* files, and`git check-ignore -v` matches both new rules (`.gitignore:62`, `.gitignore:63`). |

| P-016 | `apps/web/src/services/storage/migrations/runner.ts`                                     | Two call-shape corrections, made as **one** patch: `new IndexedDBAdapter<ProjectRecord>("video-editor-projects", "projects", 1)` → the options-object form the constructor declares, and `projectsAdapter.set(projectId, result.project)` → `set({ key: projectId, value: result.project })`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | `inherited-defect-repair` — "A call passing positional arguments into an options-object function is a defect", and every enumerated site gets a _repaired_ or _harmless_ verdict, never a third outcome. `browser-persistence-boundary` — "The migration runner operates on the real projects database" and "No database named after an undefined value exists". The two sites are one patch on purpose: `set` is unreachable while the constructor is broken, so repairing the constructor alone would convert a silent no-op into a `DataError` (`store.put({ id: undefined })` against keyPath `"id"`) on the first legacy project — strictly worse than the defect.                                                                                                                                                               | **Behaviour change accepted and intended.** Before: destructuring a string left `dbName`/`storeName` `undefined`, `indexedDB.open(undefined)` succeeded against a database literally named `"undefined"`, `getAll()` returned `[]`, and the runner concluded there was nothing to migrate. Recorded in both hosts' pre-repair `ledger-<host>.json` → `persistedDatabases` as `{ name: "undefined", stores: [{ store: "undefined", rows: 0 }] }`; absent from both post-repair ledgers. Runtime proof the write-back path now works: the seeded-legacy probe (`apps/vite-example/tests/probe/legacy-migration.pw.ts`) fails pre-repair with "Expected 31, Received 1" and passes post-repair on **both** hosts. Type gate: this file's two `TS2554` entries are listed as resolved by `script/check-type-baseline.mjs` (13 → 3).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| P-017 | `apps/web/src/services/storage/migrations/v1-to-v2.ts`                                   | Three call-shape corrections to `new IndexedDBAdapter(...)`: the per-scene legacy timeline read, the main-scene fallback read, and the legacy media-metadata read.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | `inherited-defect-repair`, as P-016. `browser-persistence-boundary` — "Legacy per-project databases are read before they are deleted", "Legacy timeline data is carried forward, not discarded", and "Legacy media metadata informs the migrated project".                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | **Behaviour change accepted and intended.** Before: all three reads resolved against the `"undefined"` database, so a v1 project's tracks were treated as absent and `mediaTypesById` was always empty — and `deleteLegacyTimelineDbs` then deleted the real legacy databases, dropping the data. The probe asserts recovered _content_, never the absence of the legacy databases, because a working delete and a broken read look identical otherwise: post-repair, project A's migrated record contains all three recovered legacy elements (media, text, audio) and project B's contains the element reachable **only** through the main-scene fallback. The media-metadata read is evidenced separately, by type resolution: A's recovered element becomes `image` because its legacy metadata says so, while B's, which has no metadata, keeps the `video` fallback. Additive-migration policy re-checked: `loadV1ToV2Context` reads before `transformProjectV1ToV2`, and `deleteLegacyTimelineDbs` runs only when `!result.skipped` and only after the transform, so `migrations/AGENTS.md`'s "do not delete, rename, or replace persisted data" ordering still holds — the repair makes the read real for the first time, it does not reorder anything. A project already at `CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION` is asserted byte-unchanged by a second probe case. |
| P-018 | `apps/web/src/stickers/providers/index.ts`                                               | `stickersRegistry.register(provider.id, provider)` → `register({ key: provider.id, definition: provider })`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | `inherited-defect-repair`, as P-016. This is the site that **falsifies** the "every positional-argument site is on the storage migration path" hypothesis, and the one with the broadest user-visible consequence.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | **Behaviour change accepted and intended, and it is the most visible of the six.** Before: `definitions.set(undefined, undefined)` succeeded, `has("logos")` stayed false, so all three providers overwrote the same `undefined` entry and the registry ended with exactly one, `undefined → undefined` — zero providers registered. Measured pre-repair (`stickers-before.png`, `stickers-before-vite.json`): the panel reads "No stickers found" / "No stickers available yet.", zero sticker images, zero sections, and **zero** page or console errors on either search path — the fault is swallowed everywhere the panel can reach it. Measured post-repair (`stickers-after.png`, `stickers-after-vite.json`): 18 sticker previews, sections "Recently used" / "Flags" / "Shapes", search returning results, and one persisted recent sticker id per provider (`flags:us`, `logos:github`, `shapes:circle`) surviving a reload — which is what proves each provider is registered under **its own** id, since `sanitizeRecentStickers` keeps an id only when `registry.has(providerId)` is true. Corroborates S01's `evidence/03-stickers-flags.png`. Type gate: this file's `TS2554` entry is listed as resolved.                                                                                                                                         |
| P-019 | `apps/web/src/actions/keybinding.ts`                                                     | Added `isShortcutKey(value: string): value is ShortcutKey` and the `MODIFIER_KEY_COMBINATIONS` table it derives from. No existing declaration altered.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | `inherited-defect-repair` — "An imported symbol is defined somewhere in the repository": `actions/keybindings/persistence.ts` imports this guard (line 2, used at 62 and 108) and nothing in the repository defined it. Placed beside the existing `isKey` guard, which is the precedent for this shape; a single positional subject is the _conforming_ form for a type predicate under `opencut/prefer-object-params`, which carves type predicates out explicitly rather than treating them as an exception.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Additive: no existing export changed, so no existing caller can be affected. The behaviour is taken from the committed test rather than invented — `keybindings/__tests__/persistence.test.ts` requires `space` accepted and `shift+bogus` rejected. Drift is a compile error: `MODIFIER_KEY_COMBINATIONS` is annotated `Record<ModifierKeys, true>`, so a missing **or** an unknown modifier fails to compile, where a `satisfies` on an array would catch only the second — and it is the missing one that would silently make a valid shortcut unrecognised. Verified by `bun test`: the 9 tests in that file went from never loading to loading and passing, and the file's `TS2724`/`TS2345` baseline entries are listed as resolved.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| P-020 | `apps/web/src/actions/definitions.ts`                                                    | Added `isActionWithOptionalArgs(value: string): value is TActionWithOptionalArgs`, the local `TActionWithRequiredArgs` type, and the `ACTIONS_WITH_REQUIRED_ARGS` table. No existing declaration altered.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | `inherited-defect-repair`, as P-019: `persistence.ts` imports this guard from `@/actions` (line 4, used at 62 and 111) and nothing defined it. Sited here because this is the only module holding the runtime `ACTIONS` table, and it reaches `@/actions` through the existing `export * from "./definitions"`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Additive. Derived from `ACTIONS`, **not** from `ACTION_DEFAULT_SHORTCUTS` — the committed test pins this by asserting `stop-playback` is accepted despite being absent from the defaults table. Drift is a compile error: `ACTIONS_WITH_REQUIRED_ARGS` is annotated `Record<TActionWithRequiredArgs, true>` where `TActionWithRequiredArgs = Exclude<TAction, TActionWithOptionalArgs>` is computed, never listed, so the moment an action's `TActionArgsMap` entry stops including `undefined` the literal no longer type-checks. **Resolves OQ1 in the affirmative:** the two required-args actions are rejected at runtime as well as by the type, because binding one to a key would invoke it with no argument and persist a shortcut that cannot work. Verified by `bun test` as P-019, and the `persistence.ts` `TS2345` pair disappeared through narrowing — no cast or assertion was added (`@typescript-eslint/no-unsafe-type-assertion` is an error in this scope). **Reachability recorded rather than overstated:** `persistence.ts` has exactly one importer in the whole repository — its own test file — so this repair changes no application behaviour.                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| P-021 | `package.json`                                                                           | `"opencut-wasm": "^0.2.10"` → `"opencut-wasm": "file:./rust/wasm/pkg"`. **Amended (review M2/M4/M5):** `build:wasm` now runs `node script/build-wasm.mjs` instead of calling `wasm-pack` directly; `dev:wasm`'s watch command gains `&& bun install`; new `check:wasm` script; new `preinstall` guard. **Amended again (re-review N2):** `check:wasm` now runs both wasm gates, `check-wasm-source.mjs` **and** `check-wasm-paths.mjs`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | S02 §3.9 / §4.1(c) and capability `self-built-wasm-artifact` — "the `opencut-wasm` specifier SHALL resolve to the artifact produced by building `rust/wasm` from this repository … A copy obtained from a package registry SHALL NOT satisfy the specifier." Upstream is archived, so the registry package can never gain the teardown and handle-keyed exports §3.7 requires; building from source is the only origin that can. The `file:` form was chosen over committing the built artifact (design D-A2, rejected: it contradicts P-002's "generated output is checked, not committed", adds ~3.2 MB of binary per rebuild, and makes the Rust source stop being the review surface at the moment it starts mattering) and over aliasing in the two build configs (D-A3, rejected: `tsc` would still read the registry `.d.ts`). | **No behaviour change, and that is the whole claim.** The self-built artifact corresponds to published `0.2.10`: `opencut_wasm.d.ts`, `opencut_wasm.js` and `package.json` byte-identical, **638 exported symbols identical** with zero differing lines touching an `export`, same version — measured on both sides of the P-023 licence addition and recorded in `UPSTREAM.md` § WASM rebuild correspondence. No export added, removed or changed. Both Hosts build with **no** build-config change (design D-B's hypothesis, tested rather than assumed), and both emit the 3,253,931-byte self-built `.wasm` in place of the 3,037,899-byte published one. Parity unchanged on both Hosts against the recorded baseline. `check-type-baseline.mjs`: 3 diagnostics, `PASS`, unmoved.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| P-022 | `apps/web/package.json`                                                                  | `"opencut-wasm": "^0.2.10"` → `"opencut-wasm": "file:../../rust/wasm/pkg"`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | As P-021. **`opencut-wasm` is declared in two manifests, not one** — the Slice Plan's touch set named only the root. Both must move together: measured on bun 1.2.2, `apps/web` installs its **own** `node_modules/opencut-wasm` rather than following the root's, so leaving this entry on the registry range would have given the two Hosts different artifacts while both built green.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Same evidence as P-021. The re-install rule it states was measured, not assumed: bun 1.2.2 installs a `file:` dependency as **hard links** (verified by inode identity across `rust/wasm/pkg`, `node_modules/opencut-wasm` and `apps/web/node_modules/opencut-wasm`). wasm-bindgen rewrites most files in place so those propagate, but `wasm-opt` **replaces** `opencut_wasm_bg.wasm`, breaking that one link. Observed directly after the P-023 rebuild: the resolved `.wasm` was the **4,272,877-byte pre-`wasm-opt` intermediate** while every other file was current — a functionally valid module ~1 MB oversized, which nothing at runtime would have surfaced. `script/check-wasm-source.mjs` caught it, and a plain `bun install` (289 ms) repaired it. **Amended (review M4/M5):** now records that `bun dev:wasm` performs the re-install itself, and that builds route through `script/build-wasm.mjs` for path remapping.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |

The build-step change is **M5**: calling `wasm-pack` directly would have produced a differently-provenanced artifact in CI than locally, with the path remapping applied to one and not the other.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Step ordering asserted mechanically rather than by eye, by the check itself: it now verifies that a root npm script invokes it **and** that `bun-ci.yml` runs it after `bun install`, and fails otherwise. Both directions negative-controlled — deleting the CI step yields `FAIL … bun-ci.yml has no step running script/check-wasm-source.mjs` (exit 1); moving the step before `bun install` yields `FAIL … runs it BEFORE \`bun install\`, so it checks an uninstalled tree`. The workflow was restored byte-identical after each control. **Not verified by an actual CI run** — this branch is not pushed, so the ordering claim rests on the file assertion, not on observed CI output. **Amended (re-review N2):** a second gate step, "Verify the redistributed wasm discloses no build-machine paths", runs `node script/check-wasm-paths.mjs`after the first. Without it the path remapping was convention, not enforcement — an artifact from a direct`wasm-pack build`passed every gate including CI. The wiring assertion covers exactly these two current scripts; a future wasm gate must be added explicitly to the check's`GATED` list and to package/CI wiring.                                                                                                                                                                               |

Concurrent sessions additionally require one context owner: a second initialization cannot replace the adapter beneath a compositor created by the first, and disposal cannot resurrect a runtime from an abandoned future — including same-tick abort before the Promise is first polled.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | `cargo check -p opencut-wasm --target wasm32-unknown-unknown`; twelve focused Rust state tests; generated `initializeGpu(): Promise<void>` plus provider declarations compile structurally against local copies of the C1 contracts; the real-browser package probe covers zero-yield and yielded dispose-during-init, concurrent init + live handle, failed concurrent init, real WebGL, null before/after teardown, keyed release and teardown refusal. In that mode it enables async WebAssembly, optionally aliases the neutral proof control to a React-bearing control, attaches an exact-root emitted-closure collector only to the Node server compilation, and leaves standalone output disabled so the proof server can execute the collected output. The collector also mirrors compiler-emitted server WASM bytes to the runtime lookup location inside the same validated output root. With `OPENCUT_C7_HEADLESS_PROOF` absent, no `webpack` callback is installed and ordinary standalone output remains selected. | C7 `headless-editing` requires an attributable per-entry Next closure, a real React sensitivity control, executable semantic proof, and an independently protected ordinary Next Host. Next's aggregate route/NFT/source-map inventories cannot establish exact-root outgoing reachability or actual chunk ownership. | Focused collector controls pass `4/4`, including zero exact-root chunks, concatenated ownership, wrong-entrypoint ownership, and truncated emitted bytes. A normal root-base `next build` selected Next 16.1.3 Turbopack, completed all 19 routes, and the unchanged protected Next editing-parity scenario passed. The exact baseline/current WebGPU comparison and ordinary WebGL job also confirm the conditional proof hook does not explain the inherited C3 migration-observation race. Fresh C7 clean/control proof artifacts and independent re-review remain delivery gates rather than claims in this row. |
